Gharib","slug":"anthony-gharib","contributor":{"id":3959,"description":"Anthony Gharib"}}],"keywords":["news","NFL","Los Angeles Chargers","Anthony Gharib"],"story":"<p>LOS ANGELES -- Amid the franchise's first 0-5 start since 2003, <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"84caf62b-c753-71ea-a787-44fd224a4294\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/team/_/name/lac/los-angeles-chargers\">Chargers</a> coach Jim Harbaugh said Monday that he and the team's ownership group are \"in this fight together\" to turn the season around.</p>\n<p>When asked if he feels any pressure from ownership, Harbaugh said: \"John Spanos, Dean Spanos, Susie Spanos, the whole Spanos family. It's shoulder to shoulder where we're in this fight together.\"</p>\n<p>He emphasized that he's \"hell-bent\" on reversing the course and returning the franchise to winning ways. The Chargers have faltered after coming into the season off back-to-back 11-6 seasons.</p>\n<p>Being a former 14-year NFL pro could offer Harbaugh the experience of knowing what it's like to be in the locker room during a poor stretch. During his final season as a player, he played for the then-San Diego Chargers, who went 1-15 after an 0-5 start.</p>\n<p>How he handles a locker room under similar circumstances prompted a simple message.</p>\n<p><alsosee></p>\n<p>\"Keep fighting. Keep being a leader. Keep being a competitor. Keep being the believer that you've been and even more,\" he said.</p>\n<p>Harbaugh the player might have been able to navigate this run, but for Harbaugh the coach, this is uncharted territory.</p>\n<p>Dating back to last season, the Chargers have lost eight games in a row. It's the longest losing streak of Harbaugh's head-coaching career, college or professional, according to ESPN Research.</p>\n<p>He isn't tapping into anything extra to turn the tide.</p>\n<p>\"I think one of the things that we were all put on this Earth to do is to handle both success and handle loss and to do that with the same passion and enthusiasm,\" Harbaugh said. \"And to show that, that you belong. That it can be turned around. There is winning football and that's what we're doing as a team.\"</p>\n<p>For Harbaugh, his confidence in shifting the season's momentum lies with the group at his disposal. Injuries, though, continue to affect the Chargers, specifically in the offensive line and wide receivers rooms.</p>\n<p>Tackle <a data-player-guid=\"88c4051e-7340-34c7-a248-ca77a870b0fa\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4683487/joe-alt\">Joe Alt</a> is still undergoing testing for his neck injury, while left guard <a data-player-guid=\"466e2e49-9785-9bfa-0779-7891f7bc3fe4\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4040842/kayode-awosika\">Kayode Awosika</a> (fibula) is \"healing and rehabbing to get back as soon as he can,\" Harbaugh said. Four rookies started on the line against <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"51042145-e3c1-0694-ae0d-f720342ff8fc\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/team/_/name/den/denver-broncos\">Denver</a> on Sunday.</p>\n<p>Wide receiver <a data-player-guid=\"ea99dfb4-efbc-32ae-aefe-267c745f722b\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4612826/ladd-mcconkey\">Ladd McConkey</a> suited up after missing all of practice the previous week due to a foot injury. However, he did not play and stood on the sideline in uniform without his helmet. Harbaugh said McConkey was \"an emergency situation\" and will be worked into practice this week. Wide receiver <a data-player-guid=\"33a8b56f-7466-3118-bccb-5ebff76f4590\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4686612/tre-harris\">Tre' Harris</a> picked up a hamstring injury in the game and is now week-to-week. That means all the first- and second-string wide receivers are dealing with nicks: McConkey, <a data-player-guid=\"84bbb266-ee47-3828-9628-a26e48c152c8\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4429025/quentin-johnston\">Quentin Johnston</a> (chest), Harris and <a data-player-guid=\"66dd6dcb-1022-31ac-8fef-604e55d9bbbb\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4685553/brenen-thompson\">Brenen Thompson</a> (quadriceps).</p>\n<p>The Chargers' 23-17 loss to the Broncos on Sunday was one that Harbaugh blamed himself for <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/story/_/id/50163352/chargers-harbaugh-laments-momentum-swinging-4th-call\" target=\"_blank\">due to a fourth-and-1 call in the fourth quarter</a>. He was still upset about it and apologized Monday.</p>\n<p>\"I really have the same feeling that I had last night after the game when I talked to you all. I still feel like those two fourth-down decisions, especially the one fourth-down decision, did not put our team in the best chance to win that game,\" Harbaugh said. \"I regret that. I'm even sorry about that. I don't say I'm sorry unless I think I'm wrong about something and I sincerely think I was.\"</p>\n<p><photo2></p>\n<p>The decision proved costly as the Broncos scored five plays later to tie the game and steal momentum. Quarterback <a data-player-guid=\"ec880cbc-1490-cdae-548f-f844104974ae\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4038941/justin-herbert\">Justin Herbert</a> then turned it over on the Chargers' next two possessions before a sack ended the game on their final drive. Meanwhile, the Broncos scored on three straight drives to close out a victory.</p>\n<p>Herbert is up to a league-high eight interceptions, making uncharacteristic plays and throws. He also had a costly fumble in the fourth quarter against Denver.</p>\n<p>Harbaugh backed his quarterback, further highlighting the players who give him confidence the season can be salvaged.</p>\n<p>\"The way [Herbert] competes, it's like few others I've ever seen. determined to turn this season around.\"</p>\n<p>Los Angeles will need some of that and more as it faces the undefeated <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"f68f2343-8ceb-7a02-740d-af6338be21d2\" settling into parts of the country and lingering heat beginning to ease elsewhere, players have another opportunity to switch up their game-day looks. <alsosee></p>\n\n\n<p>California's early-October heat wave has weakened, while cooler conditions in the Northeast and a hurricane that brought heavy rain to parts of the South made for a mixed bag across the country this weekend. That leaves room for everything from lightweight fits to layered looks as players make their stadium entrances.</p>\n\n\n<p>Whether they're embracing the season with jackets and sweaters or sticking to their signature styles, NFL players are bringing another round of personality to the tunnel in Week 5.</p>\n\n\n<p><img src=\"http://a.espncdn.com/i/infographics/20170710_nfl_fpr/charts/_end_rule.png\" width=\"100%\"><br>\n</p>\n<h2>Monday night style</h2>\n<br>\n<br>\nMonday evenings in Los Angeles call for comfort wear, especially with temperatures in the mid-60s. But comfort wear is where players and able to showcase their creativity and style. Rivers led the way in a blue Derrick Rose <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"8c0dccae-9569-5519-52fb-e5af48e13d65\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/mens-college-basketball/team/_/id/235/memphis-tigers\">Memphis Tigers</a> jersey, baggy denim shorts and wheat Timberland boots, while Martin sported Penny Hardaway's pinstriped <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"74c23102-8c27-c8e6-ec1b-8a8fd4d42554\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nba/team/_/name/orl/orlando-magic\">Orlando Magic</a> jersey and Jackson repped Carmelo Anthony and the <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"61719eb2-11c3-4e3d-90c3-0a1319fd850b\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nba/team/_/name/ny/new-york-knicks\">New York Knicks</a>. data-clubhouse-guid=\"8fc7b962-95e1-3cb8-6a7d-b499de9ad546\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/team/_/name/ari/arizona-cardinals\">Cardinals</a> coach Mike LaFleur said he does not plan on making changes to the team's coaching duties after a 1-4 start.</p>\n<p>LaFleur said Monday, after a <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/game/_/gameId/401872991/lions-cardinals\">45-21 loss</a> to the <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"56caffee-178d-f6f2-a076-b277486b8c32\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/team/_/name/det/detroit-lions\">Detroit Lions</a>, that he will not replace himself as the offensive playcaller or defensive coordinator Nick Rallis, who calls the defensive plays.</p>\n<p>\"I mean, obviously, my job is to see the issues and handle the issues and have the tough conversations,\" LaFleur said. \"So, if I felt like changes were needed for us to help us win this week, then I'm going to do that. It's my responsibility, but I don't think that gives us the best chance to win this week.</p>\n<p>\"I think the best thing to do right now is reevaluate what we've done, what we've done well, because there are some bright spots, but there's also a lot of spots that we got to improve on. And I told the defense when I talked to the defense today where our changes are going to be made; again, I'll keep that in-house, and I'm fired up to see it happen.\"</p>\n<p><alsosee></p>\n<p>One potential change this week could be how the Cardinals structure their active roster for Sunday's game against the <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"2e1473b2-e269-fd7a-1137-c1edacb85986\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/team/_/name/lar/los-angeles-rams\">Los Angeles Rams</a>.</p>\n<p>LaFleur said Arizona will have a discussion Tuesday to talk about putting rookie quarterback <a data-player-guid=\"baca19aa-8fab-3a5e-9bb0-6cfcbcbc1b57\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4430841/carson-beck\">Carson Beck</a> on the active roster instead of him being the inactive emergency quarterback. The Cardinals had not had those discussions this season, LaFleur said, but added that they would consider it \"if we think that's what's best.\"</p>\n<p>LaFleur said he had \"some tough conversations\" with all three phases of the team Monday about where he thinks they can improve. He singled out the offense and defense for needing to find ways to improve.</p>\n<p>LaFleur said he watched film of the offense first, then the defense, followed by special teams, and then \"just started having conversations\" centered on one foundational question: \"How do we not allow this to happen ever again?\"</p>\n<p>Answering that has proved to be easier said than done for the Cardinals, whose defense has allowed more than 30 points in each of the past four games, all losses.</p>\n<p>LaFleur expressed confidence in his defensive players Monday, saying he has the \"right guys on defense to play better\" because he has seen glimpses of it at times in the first five weeks. Arizona has seven sacks in its past two games, its best two-game stretch since Weeks 12 and 13 of the 2024 season, when the Cardinals had 10 in two games.</p>\n<p>The issue, LaFleur said, is that he hasn't seen the defensive production consistently.</p>\n<p>\"I told them that today, but we got to help them with that, whether that's providing the clarity, whether it's making it easier, what have you,\" LaFleur said. \"And we got to dive into that hard over these next two days.</p>\n<p>\"And same thing on offense. When it's going good, it's going good, or it's a three-and-out machine, and there's really nowhere in between right now for us. So, we got to figure out how to do all the good stuff better and then obviously, clean up those three-and-outs.\"</p>\n<p>During the past two weeks, the Cardinals have jumped out to good starts, scoring touchdowns on their first two drives in Week 4 against the <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"378600ea-2397-8364-8399-b7c1606a49a7\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/team/_/name/nyg/new-york-giants\">New York Giants</a> and a touchdown on their first drive against the Lions before slowing down.</p>\n<p>LaFleur didn't have a full explanation Monday for why that has happened two weeks in a row, although he said there are \"some reasons why we think that's happening.\"</p>\n<p>\"That's the million-dollar question that we're trying to figure out right now,\" he said.</p>\n<p>What those reasons are, LaFleur said, will be kept \"a little bit in-house,\" but added that they'll be addressed.</p>\n\n","allowAMP":true,"allowAds":true,"allowComments":true,"allowCommerce":true,"allowContentReactions":true,"allowSearch":true,"byline":"Josh Florio"],"story":"<p><photo1></p>\n<p>Whom should you start? Whom should you sit? To help you set your fantasy football lineups and avoid starting an offensive player who won't be in the lineup, we'll post fantasy-relevant updates and analysis here as NFL teams release their official inactives lists. </p>\n<p><em><strong>Official Sunday inactives should begin coming in approximately 90 minutes before the scheduled kickoff times: around 11:30 a.m. ET for the early games and 2:30 p.m. ET for the late-afternoon games.</strong></em></p>\n<p><em>Refresh often for the latest information.</em></p>\n<hr>\n<p align=\"center\"><strong><i>Don't forget to check these out: </i><a href=\"https://www.espn.com/fantasy/football/story/_/id/50116012/fantasy-playbook-projections-lineup-locks-week-5-shadow\">Mike Clay's Playbook</a></strong> | <strong><a href=\"https://www.espn.com/fantasy/football/story/_/id/40699880/fantasy-football-injuries-position-battles-nfl-news-analysis\">Week 5 Buzz</a></strong> <br>\n<strong><a href=\"https://www.espn.com/fantasy/football/story/_/page/FFWeeklyPlayerRank26main-49797082/fantasy-football-rankings-2026-qb-rb-wr-te-dst\">Week 5 Rankings</a> | <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/fantasy/football/story/_/id/50109391/fantasy-football-rankings-best-worst-matchup-qb-rb-wr-te\">Matchup rankings for Week 5</a></strong></p>\n<hr>\n<h2>Biggest concerns on Monday Night</h2>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/buf.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"f90765ad-da8a-9a88-8beb-568c2812636d\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/3915416/dj-moore\">DJ Moore</a>, WR, Bills (shoulder):</strong> Moore is <strong>out</strong> for Monday Night Football against the Rams. It is his first time missing a game due to injury since 2020. That leaves <a data-player-guid=\"55041575-ba08-38c9-9261-b55db2c39071\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4635008/keon-coleman\">Keon Coleman</a>, <a data-player-guid=\"5a44a894-8c06-ad6f-6e7a-eacb100671bb\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4373678/khalil-shakir\">Khalil Shakir</a>, <a data-player-guid=\"cda8d5bd-c979-3dff-b1a6-a5f026926186\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4683153/skyler-bell\">Skyler Bell</a> and called up from the practice squad, <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/3122168/trent-sherfield\">Trent Sherfield</a> as the Bills receivers with <a data-player-guid=\"9cca6401-3c43-0d5f-a97e-cbb699771196\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4242433/joshua-palmer\">Joshua Palmer</a> also out this week. I would prefer to play Coleman of the bunch as he brings more upside. Coleman has been the Bills most productive receiver when Moore's missed time. That includes last week when he went for 116 yards and a touchdown. However, Shakir, who was the Bills top target in 2025 before Moore arrived, is a safer floor play. Those in deep leagues who rolled the dice on Moore may have no choice but to take a shot on Bell. This also means more volume for <a data-player-guid=\"d4652b30-6ba5-b261-e946-eb83b36a9b88\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4385690/dalton-kincaid\">Dalton Kincaid</a>, who likely will be the top target for <a data-player-guid=\"237b9f2a-4701-8fc3-60eb-250978af9893\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/3918298/josh-allen\">Josh Allen</a> tonight.</p>\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2>From earlier in Week 5</h2>\n<h3>Players in London</h3>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/phi.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"de06715d-5216-76ee-f063-dabb342c4225\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/3121023/dallas-goedert\">Dallas Goedert</a>, TE, Eagles (knee): </strong>Goedert was able to return to practice this week, albeit in a limited fashion. Still, he <strong>is active</strong> for Week 5. He should operate as the top target for <a data-player-guid=\"caceb80c-6350-a107-9fcf-7c7bd1b4edd8\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4040715/jalen-hurts\">Jalen Hurts</a>, especially in the red zone. Some names to play Goedert over this week are <em><strong><a data-player-guid=\"6fb46cf5-4ad3-ce1a-5625-0891aae50429\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4430539/brenton-strange\">Brenton Strange</a>, <a data-player-guid=\"6713c2d0-5594-f7b2-a1bc-f2a53b907fc0\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/3117256/dalton-schultz\">Dalton Schultz</a> and <a data-player-guid=\"a1368d48-6e19-3f5e-8ccb-69e7e93dedac\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4429086/michael-mayer\">Michael Mayer</a>.</strong></em></p>\n<h3>Players with early starts</h3>\n<p><strong>ACTIVE</strong></p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/cin.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"f9be91fa-7d8d-811b-6452-b14d1a1feb2c\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4362628/jamarr-chase\">Ja'Marr Chase</a>, WR, Bengals (concussion):</strong> Chase was able to practice this week, so it's no surprise to see him <strong>active</strong> for Sunday. Obviously, since Chase is playing, you're starting him. <strong><a data-player-guid=\"f6207352-946a-a75a-aff5-28f316536d4d\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4239993/tee-higgins\">Tee Higgins</a> </strong>(adductor) was a DNP to start the week but returned to action in full on Friday. He is also <strong>active </strong>and should be started by fantasy managers.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/lv.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"fb81ea34-8566-3bda-9997-f9bf0a4ba991\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4890973/ashton-jeanty\">Ashton Jeanty</a>, RB, Raiders (ankle/foot):</strong> The Raiders' star RB popped up on the injury report Thursday after logging a limited session. He did not practice on Friday but is <strong>active</strong> for Week 5. Since Jeanty is playing for the Raiders, he should start for your fantasy football team.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/ne.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"819826d3-6398-f473-46df-252be8a68036\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4569173/rhamondre-stevenson\">Rhamondre Stevenson</a>, RB, Patriots (knee): </strong>Stevenson was able to log limited practice sessions this week and is <strong>active</strong> for Week 5. Expect the Patriots' backfield to remain a split. Stevenson is the \"safer floor\" option, with <a data-player-guid=\"714eed1f-95d7-3242-83da-3b34074b4424\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4432710/treveyon-henderson\">TreVeyon Henderson</a> being the \"home run-hitting\" play against a Raiders defense that has allowed explosive runs to RBs at the sixth-highest rate.</p>\n<p><strong><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/no.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><a data-player-guid=\"488f79c1-5040-ac01-30bb-aa9c200d3c92\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/3054850/alvin-kamara\">Alvin Kamara</a>, RB, Saints (back):</strong> Kamara is <strong>active</strong> for Week 5. He should once again operate as the Saints' lead back. While he needs catches and touchdowns to make up for his inefficiency, volume has him in play as an RB3/flex option.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/nyg.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"> <strong><a data-player-guid=\"0be760c4-3eb7-338a-9b0b-20de5895543c\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4696981/cam-skattebo\">Cam Skattebo</a>, RB, Giants (shoulder): </strong>Skattebo was limited at practice, wearing a red noncontact jersey to start the week. Still, he is <strong>active</strong> for Sunday's game. Skattebo is one of the safest bets for volume each week and should be started as an RB2, even in a tough matchup on paper. Plus, with bad weather potentially in the forecast, he could even see an uptick in usage.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/nyg.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"> <strong><a data-player-guid=\"b36df804-8242-754d-43b1-9dec06fafcaf\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4361050/isaiah-likely\">Isaiah Likely</a>, TE, Giants (knee/groin): </strong>Likely was limited to start the week but is indeed <strong>active</strong> for Week 5. Likely has seen a 33% target share with <a data-player-guid=\"57af2581-cec9-d5a3-2afe-7a719925f78b\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/2969939/jameis-winston\">Jameis Winston</a> under center. That duo is coming off their best game together. Plus, the Commanders have allowed the most passing touchdowns in the league. It's a good setup for Likely, who is the Giants' top red zone option. He should be started with confidence.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/nyg.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"> <strong><a data-player-guid=\"44a2174b-1d11-3acf-8e75-fe0c13db47a6\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4595348/malik-nabers\">Malik Nabers</a>, WR, Giants (knee): </strong>Nabers sat out Wednesday's practice because of soreness in his knee but is<strong> active </strong>for Week 5. This seems like something fantasy managers need to get used to, as midweek rest days could become a frequent sight this season. Nabers is coming off his best game of the season and should comfortably be started this week.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/ten.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"58f8add5-078d-3663-9249-0f48f77ce044\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4871023/carnell-tate\">Carnell Tate</a>, WR, Titans (back): </strong>Tate was added to the injury report Thursday, which caused a scare in the fantasy community. However, he is <strong>active</strong> for Week 5 and should be started. He has seen his target rate continuously climb, reaching 43% in Week 4. Not only that, but 26 out of his 32 targets have come against zone coverage, which the Texans have played on 83% of their plays. Lastly, the Texans are top two in both yards and touchdowns allowed to receivers.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/was.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"f03f3238-b035-6f0e-5844-1529ca021c3a\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4426348/jayden-daniels\">Jayden Daniels</a>, QB, Commanders (elbow): </strong>Daniels has been practicing with a brace on his left elbow and is <strong>active</strong> for<strong> </strong>in Week 5. Though there is some added risk here, fantasy managers should look to get Daniels back into their starting lineups. Even if the Commanders have fewer designed runs for their star QB, the upside is too high to get away from. Daniels is a top-five QB option in Week 5.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/was.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"7c9f28d6-5924-d767-999d-582e169a5cd3\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4575131/jacory-croskey-merritt\">Jacory Croskey-Merritt</a>, RB, Commanders (groin):</strong> JCM joined seemingly all of his teammates on the injury report this week. He had been limited this week but is <strong>active</strong> for Week 5. The Giants have allowed 99 RB rushing yards per game and allow explosive runs at the third-highest rate (12.5%). With Daniels coming off his injury and bad weather in the mix, do not be surprised if the Commanders run more than usual. Plus, game script could be on his side. Croskey-Merritt is a high-upside flex option.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/was.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"> <strong><a data-player-guid=\"bcbc6499-ee45-3e7d-854a-52d76796786c\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4697815/rachaad-white\">Rachaad White</a>, RB, Commanders (shoulder): </strong>After missing Week 4, White has been able to return to the practice field. He is <strong>active </strong>for Sunday. That likely puts some restraints on JCM's fantasy value. Still, Croskey-Merritt is the preferred option of the two Commanders backs. Consider both to be flex options in a favorable matchup.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/was.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"04454e17-9294-6271-f951-c0339528e97e\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/3121422/terry-mclaurin\">Terry McLaurin</a> WR, Commanders (hamstring):</strong> McLaurin <strong>is active</strong> but will not be a \"full go\" (as reported by ESPN's Mike Garafolo). Given the fact that hamstrings run a risk of flaring up, paired with the fact that the Commanders are playing in wet and slippery conditions, it's tough to trust McLaurin. I would advise trying to get away from him if you have the capability. I'm certainly sitting him for teammate Croskey-Merritt.</p>\n<p>With <a data-player-guid=\"d6ba7023-458c-f59a-4af2-638770067518\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/2976212/stefon-diggs\">Stefon Diggs</a> not playing, the Commanders could be thin at receiver this week. <a data-player-guid=\"482f2cea-ae47-8408-0b5b-8ef27e7c3809\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/5081432/antonio-williams\">Antonio Williams</a> (eight targets last week) would be a sleeper flex option, likely operating as the top Commanders target. <a data-player-guid=\"eb564484-2114-ece2-1581-1bf179af62c8\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4360635/chig-okonkwo\">Chig Okonkwo</a>, who was limited at practice this week but will play, would be a sleeper tight end for those in need of a streaming option.</p>\n<p><strong>INACTIVE</strong></p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/min.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"7e13f346-0248-31d6-a4e7-8467003d0f8b\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4429205/jordan-addison\">Jordan Addison</a>, WR, Vikings (hamstring): </strong>Addison returned to practice this week after sitting out Wednesday. However, he is <strong>inactive</strong> today. Star wideout <strong><a data-player-guid=\"c7fb1996-2530-7f98-9f2f-572debf989f4\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4262921/justin-jefferson\">Justin Jefferson</a></strong> (ankle) was also listed as questionable earlier in the week but is, as expected, <strong>active</strong>. Addison's absence could provide a boost in volume to <a data-player-guid=\"3abf6eac-14c3-1c87-1bbf-346234d9af21\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/3886598/jauan-jennings\">Jauan Jennings</a>, although he has struggled to establish himself in Minnesota. If you take a shot on any other Viking, let it be <a data-player-guid=\"632b52a0-c5f6-fadd-3e6f-a358218982f9\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4036133/tj-hockenson\">T.J. Hockenson</a>.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/pit.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"da1e8487-d66d-6ff0-7438-e9d6e77856e0\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4038815/rico-dowdle\">Rico Dowdle</a>, RB, Steelers (toe): </strong>Dowdle is<strong> inactive</strong> for Week 5. <a data-player-guid=\"224e4c1e-625d-b1b2-b8ea-1316942fa7ac\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4569987/jaylen-warren\">Jaylen Warren</a> has thrived with Dowdle sidelined, and I would expect him to continue to do so. Warren can be started with confidence once again this week.</p>\n<hr>\n<p><inline1></p>\n<hr>\n<h3>Players with late starts</h3>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/lac.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"> <strong><a data-player-guid=\"ea99dfb4-efbc-32ae-aefe-267c745f722b\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4612826/ladd-mcconkey\">Ladd McConkey</a>, WR, Chargers (foot):</strong> Despite missing practice this week, McConkey is <strong>active</strong> for Week 5. Denver will be without their top two corners, which helps the upside case for McConkey. Still, given how banged up he has been, he comes with more risk than usual. Given that risk, along with how lackluster the Chargers' passing attack has been, I would still look to avoid McConkey -- and would rather go with someone like Arizona's <a data-player-guid=\"ca60ad30-827e-174e-fd6f-fa19c14e7130\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/3045523/kendrick-bourne\">Kendrick Bourne</a>.</p>\n<h3>Players on Sunday night</h3>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/bal.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"04e6bfd5-ecba-2d02-bcac-087f32564877\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4429615/zay-flowers\">Zay Flowers</a>, WR, Ravens (foot): </strong>Flowers is active for Week 5. Those of you who waited this out should start him. In 10 career games with <a data-player-guid=\"749ba0ed-1951-e15d-e708-5e95557294de\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4035671/tyler-huntley\">Tyler Huntley</a> he has averaged six targets, 4.6 catches and 66 yards per game. He scored four touchdowns in those games.</p>\n\n\n<hr>\n<h2>Ruled out before Sunday</h2>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/ari.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"> <strong><a data-player-guid=\"7a24e799-fc0e-395e-b740-4ecee71a3c53\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4432708/marvin-harrison-jr\">Marvin Harrison Jr</a>., WR, TBD (set to be traded):</strong> Harrison will be <strong>inactive</strong> for Sunday's game as the Cardinals have announced they are <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/story/_/id/50148005/sources-cardinals-finalizing-deal-trade-wr-marvin-harrison-jr\">in the process of finalizing a trade</a> for their underperforming receiver.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/bal.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"7d76fbb1-1c5e-d9f4-954f-cad43f720ae2\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/3916387/lamar-jackson\">Lamar Jackson</a>, QB, Ravens (ankle): </strong>The two-time MVP had his ankle rolled up last week and is <strong>in jeopardy of missing a couple of games</strong>. It's unfortunate because Jackson has been playing at a very high level. He's working a career high in yards per attempt (10.1) and completion percentage (70.8%) while averaging 58 rushing yards per game. With him out, Huntley will start for the Ravens, but he's more suited for superflex leagues. Some streaming options this week for managers in need are <em><strong><a data-player-guid=\"efcb42e5-0bef-62d1-b58b-dbc17fde2479\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/2578570/jacoby-brissett\">Jacoby Brissett</a>, <a data-player-guid=\"57af2581-cec9-d5a3-2afe-7a719925f78b\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/2969939/jameis-winston\">Jameis Winston</a> and <a data-player-guid=\"5fbab03e-2816-55ab-ae10-83b132803d9e\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/8439/aaron-rodgers\">Aaron Rodgers</a>.</strong></em></p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/chi.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"b00b9764-fa7b-326a-ba35-9676bab28b09\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4608686/kyle-monangai\">Kyle Monangai</a>, RB, Bears (thumb/toe): </strong>Monangai will be <strong>out</strong> for Week 5. ESPN's Jeremy Fowler has reported that Monangai is dealing with \"turf toe\" and could possibly even miss another week or two. <a data-player-guid=\"53a49a95-4f5b-fb94-9672-ef5787f431ee\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4259545/dandre-swift\">D'Andre Swift</a> continues to deal with a knee injury. Although he has not missed any time due to <em>his</em> injury, his fumbling woes have <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/story/_/id/50135427/that-aint-bears-dandre-swift-laments-early-season-fumbles\">caused his coach some concerns</a>. Swift should see even more volume and is a must-start fantasy RB1 with Monangai out.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/den.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"1e86c395-b340-8084-cacb-61d1eb0fa424\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4372012/pat-surtain-ii\">Pat Surtain II</a>, CB, Broncos (ankle):</strong> Neither Surtain nor <a data-player-guid=\"92678f63-04a1-3bbf-7cbe-7a151d72c1e2\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4382401/riley-moss\">Riley Moss</a> has practiced this week and both are <strong>out</strong> for Week 5. That would certainly make life easier for all Chargers pass catchers, if you're into that sort of thing.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/lac.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"> <strong><a data-player-guid=\"3ee99e70-0895-225c-d16b-feaaeddc3a0f\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4242283/rashawn-slater\">Rashawn Slater</a>, OT, Chargers (ankle): </strong>The Chargers tackle is expected to be <strong>sidelined for four to six weeks</strong> because of a high ankle sprain. This just makes it tougher to trust <a data-player-guid=\"ec880cbc-1490-cdae-548f-f844104974ae\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4038941/justin-herbert\">Justin Herbert</a> and <a data-player-guid=\"757d9056-88c7-3df1-b2e3-f6f8f186780b\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4685382/omarion-hampton\">Omarion Hampton</a>. I would look to avoid both of those players against a tough Broncos defense. Additionally, OT <a data-player-guid=\"88c4051e-7340-34c7-a248-ca77a870b0fa\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4683487/joe-alt\">Joe Alt</a> suffered a neck injury and has not practiced this week. It's very tough to trust any Chargers this week.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/lac.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"84bbb266-ee47-3828-9628-a26e48c152c8\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4429025/quentin-johnston\">Quentin Johnston</a>, WR, Chargers (chest):</strong> The Chargers receiver is <strong>out</strong> for Week 5 against the Broncos. That should lead to a bump for Harris and the other available Chargers pass catchers. Still, given their offensive woes and O-line injuries, the Chargers should be avoided by fantasy managers this week.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/ne.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"f125b093-8b6f-4d5d-8984-87d3babbc871\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/2991662/mack-hollins\">Mack Hollins</a>, WR, Patriots (calf): </strong>Hollins has not practiced this week and has been<strong> ruled out</strong> for Week 5. That makes <a data-player-guid=\"931aa964-f11a-a6ef-f449-c1649f193837\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4361432/romeo-doubs\">Romeo Doubs</a>, who had nine targets last week, even more appealing. Doubs should be started over <em><strong><a data-player-guid=\"7f300cb7-f0a1-a43a-d0b0-f8a57bf04eb3\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/3126486/deebo-samuel-sr\">Deebo Samuel Sr</a>., <a data-player-guid=\"26f43986-7674-3ef9-b523-f3f66dbd16a4\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4431492/roman-wilson\">Roman Wilson</a>, <a data-player-guid=\"0385d73f-aa41-3619-868a-98942554ba97\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4870653/kc-concepcion\">KC Concepcion</a> and <a data-player-guid=\"32a202cd-dda5-e1c7-1c11-569436ff5230\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/3128429/courtland-sutton\">Courtland Sutton</a>.</strong></em></p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/phi.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"94486c18-dd3e-6081-a3fe-4a26b71ddcdc\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/3929630/saquon-barkley\">Saquon Barkley</a>, RB, Eagles (hamstring):</strong> Barkley exited last week's game and is <strong>out </strong>for Week 5 because of a hamstring injury. The Eagles also placed RB <a data-player-guid=\"09866400-3945-31d3-b7a4-aaec42a264a4\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4429013/tank-bigsby\">Tank Bigsby</a> on IR this week, leaving <a data-player-guid=\"13cb89a3-9b63-310f-bc76-fbd92c1fee83\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4431545/will-shipley\">Will Shipley</a> as their lead back. Fantasy managers may need to use Shipley this week but should look to avoid him if possible. He faces the Jaguars, a team that has allowed the fewest RB rushing yards per game -- and just one rushing touchdown all season.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/phi.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"79fe15c5-5f05-f939-c4f5-c55466521a50\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4241478/devonta-smith\">DeVonta Smith</a>, WR, Eagles (hamstring): </strong>Smith is <strong>out</strong> for the Eagles' London matchup against the Jaguars. That leaves <a data-player-guid=\"323bc065-b098-29fc-a946-25c0c2752929\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4428850/dontayvion-wicks\">Dontayvion Wicks</a> and <a data-player-guid=\"d7e2315a-4185-3bb5-846b-9e6acda6fa19\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4870795/makai-lemon\">Makai Lemon</a> as the team's top receivers. Still, no Eagles pass catcher reached as many as 35 yards last week and the team will now be operating without two expected starters on the offensive line. While one could opt to simply pivot to <a data-player-guid=\"de06715d-5216-76ee-f063-dabb342c4225\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/3121023/dallas-goedert\">Dallas Goedert</a>, it's probably best to seek options elsewhere to replace Smith.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/pit.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"f9ac7a55-5934-2bf7-e70a-9c1d04cac2eb\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4035687/michael-pittman-jr\">Michael Pittman Jr</a>, WR, Steelers (foot): </strong>Pittman has <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/story/_/id/50133939/steelers-receiver-michael-pittman-aggravates-foot-weeks\">aggravated a foot injury</a> and <strong>has been placed on IR</strong>. He had been off to a slow start, so fantasy managers should have already been looking in another direction. This means more run for <a data-player-guid=\"26f43986-7674-3ef9-b523-f3f66dbd16a4\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4431492/roman-wilson\">Roman Wilson</a>, who has become a Rodgers favorite of late. Wilson is a deep-league flex option in a favorable matchup.</p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/nfl/500/was.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"><strong><a data-player-guid=\"d6ba7023-458c-f59a-4af2-638770067518\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/2976212/stefon-diggs\">Stefon Diggs</a>, WR, Commanders (hamstring):</strong> Diggs, who saw nine targets last week, did not practice this week because of a hamstring injury that will keep him <strong>out</strong> for Week 5. With <a data-player-guid=\"04454e17-9294-6271-f951-c0339528e97e\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/3121422/terry-mclaurin\">Terry McLaurin</a> also up in the air, the Commanders are suddenly looking very thin at receiver. <a data-player-guid=\"482f2cea-ae47-8408-0b5b-8ef27e7c3809\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/5081432/antonio-williams\">Antonio Williams</a> (who saw eight targets last week) would be a sleeper flex option, likely operating as the top Commanders target. <a data-player-guid=\"eb564484-2114-ece2-1581-1bf179af62c8\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4360635/chig-okonkwo\">Chig Okonkwo</a>, who was limited at practice this week but appears likely to play, would be a sleeper tight end for those in need of a streaming option.</p>\n\n\n\n\n","allowAMP":true,"allowAds":true,"allowComments":true,"allowCommerce":true,"allowContentReactions":true,"allowSearch":true,"byline":"Michael F. href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/team/_/name/nyg/new-york-giants\">Giants</a> and said Monday that no one is happy with the game's botched final sequence or the team's 1-3-1 record.</p>\n<p>And while Quinn declined to say if he was now worried about his job security, he did say, \"I do know how the league goes. I'm well aware of that.\"</p>\n<p>\"Trust me, no one is pleased with this outcome and where we're at,\" Quinn said after his latest meeting with Harris. \"But we do have those hard conversations, we're honest about what we're at and what we need to fix and what we need to work on. And so I like having that communication with him.\"</p>\n<p>There are no plans to make any coaching changes, a team source with knowledge of the situation told ESPN. But this start preceded by a 5-12 season has led to such questions only two years after Quinn guided the Commanders to the NFC Championship Game.</p>\n<p>Those questions were fueled by <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/story/_/id/50162242/commanders-daniels-takes-blame-botched-final-play-ot\">what happened at the end of overtime Sunday</a>, when Washington reached the Giants' 24-yard line with 50 seconds remaining and the score tied. The offense did not end up snapping the ball for the next play until 12 seconds remained. With no timeouts left, quarterback <a data-player-guid=\"f03f3238-b035-6f0e-5844-1529ca021c3a\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4426348/jayden-daniels\">Jayden Daniels</a> dropped back to pass after a fake handoff, then started to scramble and was tackled with eight seconds to play.</p>\n<p><alsosee></p>\n<p>As Commanders players half-heartedly got back to the line, with Daniels and others slow to get off the ground, Washington ran out of time to spike the ball and stop the clock to set up a game-winning field goal attempt.</p>\n<p>Quinn said offensive coordinator David Blough called the play, but he is ultimately responsible for what transpires.</p>\n<p>\"All of that comes through me,\" Quinn said. \"Dave's the one that calls it, but I want to make sure I'm clear on my guidelines, too. This will break down in a lot of ways, so it starts with me first.\"</p>\n<p>The Commanders do have a game management coordinator in the booth, Connor Nickol, to help. But the issue, Quinn said, was the team trying to do too much. The previous play ended at 50 seconds, but the Commanders subbed in players and did not line up for the next snap until 18 seconds remained -- and the ball wasn't snapped for another six seconds.</p>\n<p>\"It took entirely too long to execute our strategy,\" Quinn said, \"from subbing to clock awareness to clocking the ball.\"</p>\n<p>Quinn said \"burning some time is the smart call\" in that situation because three field goals and an extra point had been missed in the game. The Commanders did not want to give New York much time on the clock in a worst-case scenario if they missed a field goal.</p>\n<p>Washington then ran a play-action pass with three tight ends and a receiver -- two of them ended up 14 yards downfield.</p>\n<p>Quinn said if it hadn't taken so long to run the next play, then, \"you can get back on the ball and clock it. ... Hindsight, don't sub, we can get back on the ball with the same group and do the same outcome.\"</p>\n<p>Quinn said the communication about the situation must start with him and trickle down to Blough and to Daniels. They have to remind Daniels, who has to pass the information to teammates, how many timeouts are remaining and what the offense needs to do when the play ends. But, Quinn said, these are scenarios they practice multiple times each week -- he dubs them \"winning time moments.\"</p>\n<p>\"It definitely looked like the urgency of what I expected from us in that spot wasn't there,\" Quinn said. \"That's the part that pisses you off the most.\"</p>\n<p>Quinn said he isn't worried about players losing faith in the coaching staff after Sunday. But he clearly was frustrated after the play, slamming his headset to the ground and then standing on the field at the 25-yard line and staring straight ahead for several minutes.</p>\n<p>Many fans felt the same emotion. When asked about a loss in confidence in him, Quinn said, \"I'm just as pissed. This is a really hard loss, man.\"</p>\n\n\n\n\n","allowAMP":true,"allowAds":true,"allowComments":true,"allowCommerce":true,"allowContentReactions":true,"allowSearch":true,"byline":"John coach Jeff Hafley is not considering a quarterback change heading into their Week 6 bye, but he did say his team's passing offense must improve.</p>\n<p>Speaking to reporters Monday, Hafley reiterated that <a data-player-guid=\"77d56116-fed8-b61e-d8d8-9a30107e2104\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4242512/malik-willis\">Malik Willis</a> will remain the team's starting quarterback, and he is not considering a change to backup <a data-player-guid=\"50d686f7-c99d-3aba-91a0-ebc0eb61cd1f\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4433971/kyle-mccord\">Kyle McCord</a>. However, particularly after Sunday's loss to the <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"75b8623b-8926-b411-0476-3d3602b78523\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/team/_/name/cin/cincinnati-bengals\">Cincinnati Bengals</a>, during which the Dolphins struggled to move the ball through the air, Hafley acknowledged the team must be able to pass the ball more effectively.</p>\n<p>\"Malik needs to be better, OK? And that's not the headline,\" Hafley said. \"The headline is we all need to be better, and I mean that. I thought yesterday for the first time we ran the ball really well and we moved the ball. We ran for 200 yards against a really good team. But everything in the pass game has to be better, right? It's the receivers, it's the tight ends, it's the protection. It all has to tie in, and it all has to be better.</p>\n<p>\"And that's the quarterback, that's the coaches, that's the offensive line, that's the running backs, that's the tight ends. This is a team game that we all need to be more efficient, and we need to be better. And we will work through that. I have confidence in the coaches, I have confidence in the players that we have. We will work through that and we will get better. It's been five weeks.\"</p>\n<p><photo1></p>\n<p>Willis ranks 31st in the NFL with a 56.2% completion percentage and 23rd in quarterback rating (43.9). Through five games, he has thrown for 849 yards and two touchdowns against four interceptions.</p>\n<p>The onetime third-round pick has also rushed for 186 yards and two touchdowns, albeit with five fumbles.</p>\n<p>Willis has lost fumbles in each of his past three games, which is an area Hafley said the team will focus on during the bye week.</p>\n<p>\"We got to look hard at it during the bye week,\" Hafley said. \"We talk about ball security all the time and we work on it and we've got to do a better job of putting him in those situations and he's got to have the awareness to protect the ball as well. We'll take a hard look at fixing that because we're not going to win games if we're fumbling the ball like that.\"</p>\n<p><alsosee></p>\n<p>The Dolphins' offense is the youngest in the NFL, with three rookies receiving consistent playing time. Their offensive line, which returned its top four starters from a season ago, ranks middle of the pack in terms of protection with a 50.8% pass block win rate. But Willis is the fifth-most contacted quarterback in the league, and the fourth-most sacked.</p>\n<p>He got off to a slow start in Sunday's loss, at one point throwing more interceptions than completions before ultimately finishing with 137 yards and a touchdown on 15-of-27 passing.</p>\n<p>Miami enters its bye week on a five-game losing streak to start the season and Willis said the week needs to focus on self-reflection and accountability if the team is to earn its first win since Dec. 28.</p>\n<p>\"I think we just got to watch the tape and figure it out. And that's the thing that I think Coach Haf does great,\" Willis said. \"He holds everybody accountable and he's honest with us. If we need to do better, we need to do better. And if we got to execute, we need to execute. We got to figure out a way to get guys on the same page because there's just a lot left out on the field that could be better than what it University","abbreviation":"Indiana","shortDisplayName":"Indiana"}},{"id":1095,"type":"team","uid":"s:20~l:23~t:2633","guid":"de276cc4-dc02-159f-0c1c-67ddaf02a9ea","description":"Tennessee
