of Tennessee","sportId":3170,"teamId":2633,"team":{"id":2633,"description":"University of Tennessee","abbreviation":"Tennessee","shortDisplayName":"Tennessee"}}],"keywords":["daily","NCAA Football","Football Recruiting","Texas Tech Red Raiders","Texas Tech University","Ohio State Buckeyes","The Ohio State University","Florida Gators","University of Florida","Georgia Bulldogs","University of Georgia","Texas Longhorns","University of Texas","Texas A&M Aggies","Texas A&M University","USC Trojans","University of Southern California","Alabama Crimson Tide","University of Alabama","Oregon Ducks","University of Oregon","South Carolina Gamecocks","University of South Carolina","Indiana Hoosiers","Indiana University","Tennessee Volunteers","University of Tennessee"],"story":"<p><photo1></p>\n<p>As the Dec. 2-4 early signing period enters the horizon, <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/playerrankings/_/view/rn300\">ESPN's 2027 five-star class</a> is settled.</p>\n<p>That's largely been the case since coveted running back <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261936/david-gabriel-georges\">David Gabriel Georges</a>, the last uncommitted five-star in the cycle, picked <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"de276cc4-dc02-159f-0c1c-67ddaf02a9ea\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/2633/tennessee-volunteers\" target=\"_new\">Tennessee</a> over <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"2fc3b71f-8247-22a5-f6da-df3902cbb438\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/194/ohio-state-buckeyes\" target=\"_new\">Ohio State</a> on July 22. No. 1 overall recruit <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/265222/jalen-brewster\">Jalen Brewster</a> is locked in with <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"4350bc91-1a0d-8292-bf66-9687147703f0\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/2641/texas-tech-red-raiders\" target=\"_new\">Texas Tech</a>. Ditto for top-ranked quarterback <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261992/elijah-haven\">Elijah Haven</a> (<a data-clubhouse-guid=\"258022c1-fed4-a0ce-c017-1f853c81d1a8\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/333/alabama-crimson-tide\" target=\"_new\">Alabama</a>), No. 1 running back <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261560/kemon-spell\">Kemon Spell</a> (<a data-clubhouse-guid=\"4351fef8-fe69-53b1-ea57-72684b36ec35\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/61/georgia-bulldogs\" target=\"_new\">Georgia</a>) and all four of ESPN's five-star offensive tackles. All in, it had been a strikingly quiet fall atop the 2027 class before No. 1 overall wide receiver <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/265179/easton-royal\">Easton Royal</a> completed his seismic flip from <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"013a8bab-0dd5-f6a0-45e6-1128a124f34b\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/251/texas-longhorns\" target=\"_new\">Texas</a> to <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"fe74bfd9-c15e-7b3a-ab25-ff4e5636282e\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/57/florida-gators\" target=\"_new\">Florida</a> <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-football/story/_/id/50159015/easton-royal-top-wr-27-flips-florida-texas\">on Oct. 11</a>.</p>\n<p>Royal's recruitment is closed, at least for now. And while there is always room for late drama in recruiting, further movement within the five-star class before signing day appears unlikely.</p>\n<p>So let's break down the 2027 five-star class. For each commitment, ESPN college football and recruiting reporter Eli Lederman and scouts Craig Haubert and Tom Luginbill take a look at how the nation's top prospects landed on their college choice and what to expect from them at the next level.</p>\n\n\n<p><img src=\"http://a.espncdn.com/i/infographics/20170710_nfl_fpr/charts/_end_rule.png\" width=\"100%\"></p>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/265222/jalen-brewster\">Jalen Brewster</a>, DT</h2>\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/2641.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 1</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"4350bc91-1a0d-8292-bf66-9687147703f0\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/2641/texas-tech-red-raiders\" target=\"_new\">Texas Tech Red Raiders</a></p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: For the first time in the ESPN recruiting rankings era (dating to 2006), the nation's No. 1 overall prospect is bound for Lubbock, Texas.</p>\n<p>Brewster became the cornerstone of Texas Tech's 2027 class when he committed to the Red Raiders last October over finalists Ohio State, Texas A&M, Oregon, Oklahoma and Indiana. The son of former All-MAC offensive lineman Robert Brewster -- a third-round selection of the <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/team/_/name/dal/dallas-cowboys\">Dallas Cowboys</a> in the 2009 NFL draft -- the 6-foot-3, 310-pound Brewster has emerged as an explosive disrupter on the defensive line at Texas' Cedar Hill High School, where Texas Tech coach Joey McGuire won three state titles in 14 seasons from 2003 to 2016.</p>\n<p><alsosee></p>\n<p>Brewster remains the subject of heavy flip interest across the country. He took official visits to Indiana, SMU, LSU, Texas and Florida this spring before closing his visit slate with a trip back to Texas Tech over the weekend of June 19 amid heavy flip interest. But the expectation remains that Brewster will be difficult to pry away from the Red Raiders. -- <em>Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: Being disruptive along the defensive front was central to Texas Tech's Big 12 title and College Football Playoff run in 2025, and Brewster fits that identity perfectly. At around 310 pounds, he's a jumbo athlete who was nimble enough to also take snaps in the Wildcat formation in high school. His home is on defense where he can be a playmaker in the trenches, blending his size with an outstanding quickness, agility and power, which can allow him to anchor against double-teams while still being able to penetrate and disrupt in the backfield. There will be a year gap, but Brewster projects into a <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/4431551/lee-hunter\">Lee Hunter</a>-type role on the interior, but with even more upside as a dynamic interior pass rusher. -- <em>Haubert</em></p>\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2></h2>\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261490/dj-jacobs\">D.J. Jacobs</a>, DE</h2>\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/194.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 2</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"2fc3b71f-8247-22a5-f6da-df3902cbb438\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/194/ohio-state-buckeyes\" target=\"_new\">Ohio State Buckeyes</a></p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: Jacobs, the son of former Georgia defensive tackle David Jacobs, and the godson of ex-Bulldogs coach Mark Richt, is one of the most productive high school defenders in the 2027 class.</p>\n<p>The 6-foot-5, 225-pound edge rusher was credited with 102 total tackles and 16 sacks in his junior season last fall before he committed to Ohio State over Miami, Georgia, Texas A&M Notre Dame and Oregon on Dec. 29. A three-year starter at Georgia's Blessed Trinity High School, Jacobs will enter his senior season later this year with nearly 300 career tackles.</p>\n<p>Jacobs remains the top-ranked member of Buckeyes coach Ryan Day's latest recruiting class this spring despite persistent flip interest from Miami. If ESPN's No. 1 edge rusher ultimately signs with Ohio State later this year, Jacobs will arrive as the program's highest-rated defensive addition since <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/237456/jack-sawyer\">Jack Sawyer</a> joined as the No. 1 overall prospect in the 2021 class. <em>-- Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: At 6-foot-5 with elite length, Jacobs brings a profile that invites comparison to former Buckeyes star <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4241986/chase-young\">Chase Young</a>. Jacobs stands out with his ability to fire off the ball with an explosive first step and has the potential to wreak havoc as an edge rusher. He can continue to improve his flexibility, but despite his lean, rangy frame, Jacobs has heavy hands and good power and that will only continue to improve as he further physically matures. He also can cover ground in pursuit and plays with a relentless motor. A quiet, focused player, his desire to get better and maximize his tools came across during in-person evaluations as well, which is a good sign for long-term success. Comparing him to Young, is a high bar but Jacobs possesses the combination of length, burst and range to develop into an equally impactful type player in Columbus. <em>-- Haubert</em></p>\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/260457/maxwell-hiller\">Maxwell Hiller</a>, OT</h2>\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/57.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 3</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"fe74bfd9-c15e-7b3a-ab25-ff4e5636282e\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/57/florida-gators\" target=\"_new\">Florida Gators</a></p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: If he signs with Florida, Hiller, ESPN's No. 1 offensive tackle in the 2027 cycle, could become the program's highest-rated offensive line addition in more than a decade.</p>\n<p>Not since <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/178931/martez-ivey\">Martez Ivey</a> signed in the 2015 cycle have the Gators landed an offensive line prospect like Hiller, who became the program's highest-ranked pledge under first-year coach Jon Sumrall when he committed to Florida over Alabama, Ohio State and Tennessee on April 8. Gators offensive line coach Phil Trautwein, who began recruiting Hiller at Penn State, was central to Florida's efforts to land the 6-foot-5, 305-pound lineman from Coatesville, Pennsylvania.</p>\n<p>A two-sport standout and the brother of five-star small forward <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-sports/basketball/recruiting/player/_/id/266026/colton-hiller\">Colton Hiller</a> -- ESPN's No. 3 overall recruit in the 2028 basketball recruiting class -- Hiller not only represented a major early recruiting win for Sumrall and his staff, but a foundation pledge to build around in the 2027 cycle. <em>-- Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: The first major recruiting win for Sumrall is a notable one as Hiller projects as a plug-and-play addition and a true cornerstone lineman. He is widely viewed as the most college-ready lineman in this class, and his profile matches what the Gators need as they retool their front. He brings a <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/4870727/cayden-green\">Cayden Green</a>-type skill (Missouri) set with even more movement ability, nearly cracking 4.9 in the 40 as a sophomore while carrying SEC-ready mass. His bend, power and nasty finish make him a strong presence in the run game, where he can generate movement at the point of attack and climb cleanly to linebackers. In pass protection, he already shows elite-caliber traits: He takes proper angles, lands a quick, jolting strike and uses his feet to mirror and adjust to counters. For a program that has struggled to stack elite OLs in recent cycles (only one Gators OL has been drafted in the previous four years), Hiller is a significant recruiting statement and a potential quick contributor for this program beginning a new era. <em>-- Haubert</em></p>\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/265643/kennedy-brown\">Kennedy Brown</a>, OT</h2>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/245.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 4</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/mens-college-basketball/team/_/id/245/texas-a&m-aggies\">Texas A&M Aggies</a></p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: Texas A&M landed the highest-ranked of the program's six five-star commits this cycle when Brown announced <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-football/story/_/id/48602545/texas-lands-third-2027-five-star-ot-kennedy-brown\">his pledge on April 26</a>.</p>\n<p>The 6-foot-4, 285-pound lineman from Kingwood, Texas, has been a frequent visitor in College Station over the past two years and completed a multi-day trip with the Aggies this spring before picking Texas A&M over Florida, LSU, Tennessee and a host of other SEC contenders. Brown joins fellow tackle <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261582/mark-matthews\">Mark Matthews</a> and defenders <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261613/raylaun-henry\">Raylaun Henry</a>, <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/265138/zyron-forstall\">Zyron Forstall</a> and <a href=\"http://Offensive tackle Mark Matthews, the most-recent five-star commit, is one of four five-stars in Texas A&M's class. Still searching for their college destinations, meanwhile, are eight of the Class of 2027's most elite recruits, led by defensive backs John Meredith III (No. 2 overall) and Joshua Dobson (No. 7).\">Kamarui Dorsey</a> among the Aggies' five-star pledges in 2027. He's now the top-ranked member of the program's four-man offensive tackle class, which includes the latest commitment from Matthews (No. 10 overall), fellow in-state commits <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/262470/kaeden-scott\">Kaeden Scott</a> (No. 137 overall) and <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/266343/demarrion-johnson\">DeMarrion Johnson</a>, a four-star prospect from Tyler, Texas.</p>\n<p>Brown is the No. 2 offensive tackle in the 2027 recruiting class and a three-year starter at Kingwood High School, where he also competes in track and field. -- <em>Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: The former top player in the Class of 2027, Brown is returning from a foot injury and looking toward a dominant senior season that could place him back at the top of his class. He's one of the more physically impressive tackles with an exceptionally long wingspan (35-inch arms) and nearly 11-inch hands. Those numbers would top any offensive tackle in the first round of the 2026 NFL draft. He does not have ideal height, but his length is outstanding and he knows how to use it functionally. He is extremely agile and athletic as well while possessing incredible bend and flexibility in pass protection. Brown still has room for physical development and is coming off a significant injury. However, should he recover successfully and physically progress, he should immediately challenge for starting job in College Station. He has the physical intangibles and is very coachable and competitive. -- <em>Haubert</em></p>\n\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261992/elijah-haven\">Elijah Haven</a>, QB</h2>\n\n\n<p><photo3></p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/333.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next 300 ranking</strong>: 5</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/333/alabama-crimson-tide\">Alabama Crimson Tide</a></p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: Haven, the lone five-star quarterback in the rankings, closed a lengthy courtship with Alabama when he announced his <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-football/story/_/id/48592711/elijah-haven-no-1-overall-2027-qb-chooses-alabama\">commitment to the Crimson Tide</a> on April 25.</p>\n<p>The 6-foot-5, 220-pound prospect from Baton Rouge, Louisiana, visited Alabama multiple times last fall before returning for a visit in January and again for the program's spring game on April 12. Haven told ESPN that relationships with Crimson Tide coach Kalen DeBoer, offensive coordinator Ryan Grubb and first-year quarterbacks coach Bryan Ellis were central to his commitment over runners-up Georgia, Auburn, Florida and Kentucky.</p>\n<p>Haven was named Louisiana's Gatorade Football Player of the Year last fall after throwing for 3,931 yards and a state-record 62 passing touchdowns while leading The Dunham School to its first state title since 2004. He's one of two quarterbacks in Alabama's 2027 class alongside four-star passer <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/257087/trent-seaborn\">Trent Seaborn</a>, ESPN's No. 201 overall prospect. -- <em>Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: Haven has elite size and physical tools. He has really developed over the past two years as a pure thrower. While he's extremely athletic and an effective runner, he's not a true dual-threat, and his potential as a passer is much higher. Haven's mechanics are still raw and will require some refinement to maximize his impressive accuracy. The Tide signed five-star <a data-player-guid=\"59c704ce-c331-3779-89af-a61ab3c06fe6\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/5141629/keelon-russell\" target=\"_new\">Keelon Russell</a> in the 2025 class, but Haven has the physical talent that will be hard to keep off the field early in Tuscaloosa. His powerful arm and physical makeup are reminiscent of former Tennessee QB Joe Milton, but Haven will be a more accurate college passer. -- <em>Luginbill</em></p>\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/260573/joshua-dobson\">Joshua Dobson</a>, CB</h2>\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/2579.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 6</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/2579/south-carolina-gamecocks\">South Carolina Gamecocks</a></p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: ESPN's No. 2 cornerback was a priority local target for South Carolina coach Shane Beamer in the 2027 cycle, and the Gamecocks managed to keep Dobson home on July 1 at the end of a tight recruiting battle with Michigan and Texas A&M.</p>\n<p>Dobson, 6-foot-1, 180 pounds, is originally from Fort Mill, South Carolina, situated roughly an hour north of the Gamecocks' campus. He transferred from South Carolina's Catawba Ridge High School to Hough High School in Cornelius, North Carolina, following his junior season, but remained heavily engaged this spring with Beamer and his staff, particularly defensive backs coach Torrian Gray. Dobson narrowed his finalists to Auburn, South Carolina, Michigan and Texas A&M in June and visited all four schools before giving his pledge to the Gamecocks.</p>\n<p>If he signs later this year, Dobson will represent the program's highest-ranked signee since defensive end <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/235136/jordan-burch\">Jordan Burch</a> arrived at South Carolina as the No. 4 overall recruit in the 2020 class. -- <em>Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's Take</strong>: A long, gifted corner with elite level speed, he posted a 4.39 40 this spring. Dobson is smooth and transitions extremely quickly in and out when turning and running or closing on the ball. He can mirror without allowing separation in man-to-man, shadowing receivers out of their breaks. He tracks the deep throw well, showing a second gear to break under the ball and has outstanding hands and ball skills. He also shows good closing speed and aggressiveness in run support, and isn't just a finesse cover corner. Dobson has awareness and instincts for the position that are coveted and his speed sets him apart. He could be a guy that sees the field early based off maturity and feel for the game. -- <em>Luginbill</em></p>\n\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261560/kemon-spell\">Kemon Spell</a>, RB</h2>\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/61.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 7</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"4351fef8-fe69-53b1-ea57-72684b36ec35\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/61/georgia-bulldogs\" target=\"_new\">Georgia Bulldogs</a></p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: Spell, an ex-Penn State commit, landed as the top-ranked member of Georgia coach Kirby Smart's 2027 class in February following a multiday visit with the Bulldogs.</p>\n<p>The powerful, 5-foot-10 rusher from McKeesport, Pennsylvania, maintained his status as ESPN's No. 1 running back after totaling 1,755 rushing yards and 28 touchdowns in his junior season last fall. After initially committing to the Nittany Lions in August 2024, Spell pulled his pledge from Penn State this past October, hours after the school fired longtime coach James Franklin. Georgia emerged as an immediate contender for Spell, hosting him for a late-season visit in November and again over the first weekend in February.</p>\n<p><inline1></p>\n<p>The second trip was enough to secure Spell's commitment over Notre Dame and Penn State. With his signature later this year, the Bulldogs will have signed at least one five-star recruit in each of the past 14 cycles dating to the Class of 2014, the longest such streak of any SEC program. <em>-- Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: Georgia has thrived with compact, explosive backs. Spell, at 205 pounds, is built similarly to <a data-player-guid=\"fb81ea34-8566-3bda-9997-f9bf0a4ba991\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4890973/ashton-jeanty\">Ashton Jeanty</a> and runs with the same low center of gravity that powered former Dawgs Knowshon Moreno and Sony Michel. Spell plays bigger than his size, pinballing off contact and generating explosive power through the hole, but he also has the burst and perimeter speed to hit 22 mph and turn the corner in Mike Bobo's outside zone scheme. His background in a single wing/wing‑T offense, where he's used as both tailback and wing, should translate well to Georgia's multiple run game and screen package, allowing him to hide behind the line, slip through tiny seams and quickly get into the second level. With an SEC-ready frame and style that fits exactly how Georgia wants to run the ball, Spell projects as an early rotation back who can grow into a true every‑down option in Athens. <em>-- Luginbill</em></p>\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/265179/easton-royal\">Easton Royal</a>, WR</h2>\n\n\n<p><phtoo4></phtoo4></p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/57.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 8</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"013a8bab-0dd5-f6a0-45e6-1128a124f34b\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/57/florida-gators\" target=\"_new\">Florida Gators</a></p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: Florida sealed the biggest flip of the cycle on Oct. 11 when Royal, the nation's top-ranked wide receiver, spurned his longtime Texas pledge and flipped to the Gators, capping a months-long recruiting saga and handing first-year coach Jon Sumrall his latest high-profile recruiting win.</p>\n<p>Royal, the 6-foot, 205-pound pass catcher, is a multi-sport athlete at New Orleans' Brother Martin High School. He was credited with 2,095 all-purpose yards and 29 total touchdowns as a junior last fall, and earlier this year broke a 46-year-old Louisiana state record after posting a state title-winning time of 10.17 seconds in the 100-meter dash.</p>\n<p>On that production and raw talent, Royal became a foundational member of Longhorns coach Steve Sarkisian's incoming class upon his commitment on Nov. 29, 2025. But while Royal maintained his place as Texas' top-ranked pledge for the 10-plus months, he also continued to entertain flip interest from across the SEC, led by LSU and Florida, both of which hosted Royal on official visits in the spring.</p>\n<p>Florida's pursuit was anchored, in part, by Royal's longstanding relationship with Sumrall, who initially offered Royal as the head coach of Tulane in March 2025. The Gators continued their efforts with Royal this fall, and the breakthrough ultimately came less than 24 hours after his latest visit to Florida during the Gators' Week 6 defeat to South Carolina. With his pledge, Royal joined No. 1 offensive tackle <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/260457/maxwell-hiller\">Maxwell Hiller</a> atop Florida's incoming class, which also includes No. 6 pocket passer <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/264879/davin-davidson\">Davin Davidson</a> (No. 107 overall), athlete <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/266493/tramond-collins\">Tramond Collins</a> (No. 158) and fellow top 300 wide receivers <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/271318/elias-pearl\">Elias Pearl</a> (No. 186) and <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/266390/anthony-jennings\">Anthony Jennings</a> (No. 241).</p>\n<p>Plenty of time remains before signing day. But for now, Royal is expected to skip previously scheduled visits to Texas and LSU and plans to fully shut down his recruiting process. -- Lederman</p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: Depending on portal retention in 2027, Florida should be returning five of their top six targets including <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/5295811/vernell-brown-iii\">Vernell Brown III</a> and <a data-player-guid=\"0a02d816-b69e-38ee-8511-df962aa18096\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/5141723/dallas-wilson\" target=\"_new\">Dallas Wilson</a> but with the addition of Royal, the Gators bring in true track-verified speed to a receiver room. He's consistently sub-4.4 speed in the 40 and sub-10.4 in the 100.</p>\n<p>Royal isn't just a straight-line sprinter. He has added muscle, giving him more physicality at the line of scrimmage, and for a young player, he already shows the feet, hips and balance of a polished route runner. That combination allows offensive coordinator Buster Faulkner to use him as a formation‑flexible weapon: motioning across the formation, on jet sweeps and orbit action, or simply taking the top off the coverage outside. His practice habits and competitiveness should help him solidify a role in Gainesville, and his ability to create explosives at all three levels -- plus contribute in the return game -- makes him an ideal successor for the big-play roles that have defined the Gators passing attack. <em>-- Luginbill</em></p>\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/260680/anthony-sweeney\">Anthony Sweeney</a>, DE</h2>\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/2641.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 9</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: Texas Tech Red Raiders</p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: Texas Tech's run on elite pass-rushing talent is set to continue in the 2027 class between Brewster and Sweeney, the No. 2 defensive end in the SC Next 300.</p>\n<p>Sweeney joined Brewster as the Red Raiders' second five-star pledge this cycle on April 13 when he committed to Texas Tech after spring visits to Alabama, Auburn, LSU, Maryland, South Carolina and Tennessee. Maryland's top-ranked prospect in 2027, Sweeney totaled 34 tackles with 3.5 sacks in his junior season at Our Lady of Good Counsel last fall before announcing his plans to transfer to in-state power St. Frances Academy for his senior year.</p>\n<p>The Red Raiders' 2026 class was headlined by five-star outside linebacker/edge <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261488/ladamion-guyton\">LaDamion Guyton</a>. Along with Brewster and Sweeney in 2027, the Red Raiders' incoming defensive line class includes top 300 defensive tackles <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/264784/kadrian-redmond\">K'Adrian Redmond</a> and <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261517/khyren-haywood\">Khyren Haywood</a>. <em>-- Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: Texas Tech is building its defense around disruptive, versatile linemen, and Sweeney is a perfect complement to Brewster as well as 2026 signee Guyton up front. The 6-foot-4, 230-pound defender brings elite length and natural bend off the edge, already showing advanced savvy as a young pass rusher. He can win with his get-off and flexibility at the corner, but also has the frame to keep adding weight and power without sacrificing quickness. Sweeney's presence gives the Red Raiders another edge threat, along with Guyton that offenses must account for, freeing Brewster and Redmond to attack from the interior. <em>-- Haubert</em></p>\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/260408/jamier-brown\">Jamier Brown</a>, WR</h2>\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/194.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 10</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: Ohio State Buckeyes</p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: Brown, the longest-tenured pledge among ESPN's 23 five-star prospects in 2027, is poised to follow <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/250312/jeremiah-smith\">Jeremiah Smith</a> and <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/256276/chris-henry-jr\">Chris Henry Jr</a>. as Ohio State's third five-star wide receiver signee in the past four cycles.</p>\n<p>The 5-foot-11 wideout from Sunbury, Ohio, was the Buckeyes' first scholarship offer in the 2027 class, then became the program's first 2027 pledge when Brown committed at the end of his sophomore season in November 2024. Brown followed last fall by hauling in 73 passes for 1,009 yards and 10 touchdowns in his junior season at Wayne High School, where he spent the first three seasons of his high school career before transferring to Ohio's Big Walnut High School before his senior season in 2026. <em>--Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: Ohio State has made elite slot play a staple of its offense, and Brown brings verified 10.3/100 and 20.7/200 track times with outstanding acceleration, burst and change of direction to the passing game. He's more than a straight-line runner, consistently winning 1-on-1s with the quickness to separate underneath and the top-end speed to run past corners. As he grows more comfortable with Ohio State's concept-heavy route tree, Brown projects as a featured mismatch in the slot and on gadget touches, with added value as a dynamic return man. He has clear early-impact potential as the next explosive option in the Buckeyes' receiver room. <em>-- Luginbill</em></p>\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261582/mark-matthews\">Mark Matthews</a>, OT</h2>\n<p><photo2></p>\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/245.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next 300 ranking:</strong> 11</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to:</strong> Texas A&M Aggies</p>\n<p><strong>Background: </strong>Matthews, ESPN's top-ranked prospect from Florida in 2027, closed a fierce recruiting battle between Texas A&M and in-state Miami when he announced his commitment to the Aggies on May 15.</p>\n<p>The 6-foot-6, 290-pound lineman is a multi-year starter at left tackle and state champion basketball player at Florida's St. Aquinas High School. Matthews made a pair of unofficial visits to both Miami and Texas A&M this spring prior to his decision. His commitment gives the Aggies pledges from two of ESPN's top-three offensive tackles in the 2027 class and will hand Texas A&M coach Mike Elko a potential tackle duo for the not-so-distant future if both Matthews and Brown ultimately sign with the program later this year.</p>\n<p>Of course, there's a lot of time from mid-May to the early signing period. Hurricanes coach Mario Cristobal has invested a lot of time and energy into Matthews over the past few years, and Miami has the resources to compete financially. Don't be surprised if the in-state Hurricanes, and potentially others, linger in his recruitment. <em>-- Eli Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take:</strong> The Aggies are bringing in an outstanding pair of young bookend tackles. First they added No. 2 ranked OT Kennedy Brown and now Matthews. Matthews is a player who initially jumped out for his mobility and promising frame as a sophomore but he needed to add mass. He added a good 20-plus pounds in the past year while retaining good knee bend and agility. He still has room to get bigger in the Aggies strength and conditioning program. As a blocker, he has good body quickness and aggressiveness. He will need to work on maintaining a more consistent pad level and needs to get better at using his length and punch in pass pro to not let defenders into his frame at times. Matthews is a \"happy warrior\" type who knows when to lock-in and brings a wealth of tools. He may not be a plug-and-play addition but along with Brown could give Texas A&M an elite pair of tackles to anchor their offense over the coming seasons. <em>-- Haubert</em></p>\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261965/marcus-fakatou\">Marcus Fakatou</a>, DT</h2>\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/194.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 12</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: Ohio State Buckeyes</p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: Fakatou, a versatile, 6-foot-7, 275-pound defender, is the top-ranked prospect in California and ESPN's No. 2 defensive tackle in the 2027 class.</p>\n<p>He trimmed his finalists to Georgia, Ohio State and Texas in June, canceling official visits to Notre Dame and Oregon in the late stages of his process, and Fakatou became the Buckeyes' fourth five-star pledge in the 2027 class when he committed to the program on June 29.</p>\n<p>A reclass from the 2028 class, Fakatou was credited with 69 tackles (17 for loss) and seven sacks in his junior season at California's Orange Lutheran High School last fall. He announced plans to transfer to nearby Sierra Canyon in March, where Fakatou will spend his senior season playing alongside Texas pledge and No. 3 defensive tackle <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261547/kasi-currie\">Kasi Currie</a>. While Fakatou is classified as a defensive tackle, many Power 4 talent evaluators view his future at defensive end. -- <em>Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: Ohio State landed one of the elite big men in this class. Fakatou has a wonderful blend of size and mobility while still possessing great physical upside. At 6-foot-6 and 270 pounds, he has a big frame that seems to not even have scratched the surface of what it can develop into. For his size he moves very well, posting excellent testing numbers this past spring including a 4.73-forty and while he can use a little more flexibility work, can bend and change directions well too.</p>\n<p>The interesting aspect to follow for Fakatou is how he will fit and be best utilized within the Buckeyes scheme. With his size and room for growth and additional mass he projects to defensive tackle, and while he will take some reps inside, he predominately plays end for his high school and sees himself in that role. We feel ultimately he'll best fit as an interior presence where he can be handful with his size, power and agility but we wouldn't be surprised if he can settle in and make an impact outside much like JT Tuimoloau did. He was also a big body at close to 6-foot-5 and roughly 270 as well coming out of high school.</p>\n<p>However Ohio State ultimately deploys him, he brings versatility and the tools to be a handful versus the run and disrupt with power as a pass rusher and can be an impact presence in the trenches. -- <em>Haubert</em></p>\n\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261936/david-gabriel-georges\">David Gabriel Georges</a>, RB</h2>\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/2633.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 rankings</strong>: 13\n\n\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"de276cc4-dc02-159f-0c1c-67ddaf02a9ea\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/2633/tennessee-volunteers\" target=\"_new\">Tennessee Volunteers</a></p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: You have to go back to the Class of 2009, when the Vols landed No. 2 running back Bryce Brown, for the last time Tennessee signed a running back rated as highly as Gabriel Georges.</p>\n<p>Originally from Quebec, the nation's No. 2 running back recruit is the latest standout from a pipeline of Canadian-born running backs that includes Rutgers' <a data-player-guid=\"750a874e-c4bb-3a10-9eff-b02a9745d523\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/5209945/antwan-raymond\" target=\"_new\">Antwan Raymond</a>, ex-Oklahoma State rusher <a data-player-guid=\"7c6b0551-49b5-aa13-9e26-7db2f9dad46f\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4241416/chuba-hubbard\">Chuba Hubbard</a> and former Illinois volume carrier <a data-player-guid=\"2457b00f-3a1f-3362-a891-ac761e110cf6\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/5087012/chase-brown\" target=\"_new\">Chase Brown</a>. Gabriel Georges relocated to Baylor School in Chattanooga, Tennessee, ahead of the 2024 campaign and has rushed for 2,852 rushing yards and 41 touchdowns in two varsity seasons.</p>\n<p>Gabriel Georges closed a fierce recruiting battle between Ohio State and Tennessee with his pledge to the Vols on July 22, and he now stands as the top-ranked member of coach Josh Heupel's latest class. After signing a pair of five-stars in the 2026 cycle between quarterback <a data-player-guid=\"62229cc3-ce44-34d6-bcf8-4c7999c67a5a\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/5158973/faizon-brandon\" target=\"_new\">Faizon Brandon</a> and wide receiver <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/259405/tristen-keys\">Tristen Keys</a>, the Vols have an elite running back on the way in 2027. -- <em>Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: Tennessee returns a 1,000-yard rusher in <a data-player-guid=\"9731492f-d104-3ada-bcc2-4fc900ce6986\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/4921108/desean-bishop\" target=\"_new\">DeSean Bishop</a> but there's a high likelihood he will already have moved onto the NFL by the time Georges arrives on campus. That opens the door for the home run threat to be a feature back in Josh Heupel's offense for 2027. Georges reminds us of former USC running back Joe McKnight. He's built like a wide receiver with running back skills. He's only going to get bigger and stronger over time, which will really help him in terms of durability and power between the tackles. -- <em>Luginbill</em></p>\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/259365/caden-moss\">Caden Moss</a>, OT</h2>\n\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/194.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 14</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: Ohio State Buckeyes</p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: The 6-foot-5, 330-pound lineman from Jackson, Mississippi, committed to the Buckeyes on June 26 and stands among four five-star prospects committed to the program's 2027 class.</p>\n<p>Moss, ESPN's No. 4 offensive tackle in 2027, took official visits to LSU, Kentucky, Ohio State, Ole Miss, Oregon and Tennessee across a busy travel slate this spring. Sources told ESPN that Ole Miss and Kentucky were among the most serious contenders for his pledge alongside the Buckeyes. Now the headliner of a deep Ohio State offensive line class, Moss would represent the program's highest-ranked offensive tackle signee since <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4428991/paris-johnson-jr\">Paris Johnson Jr</a>. in the 2020 cycle. -- <em>Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: Ohio State's key contributors on the offensive line in 2026 will predominately be juniors and seniors, so with some turnover on the horizon the Buckeyes are stocking the cupboard with OL talent. Already with a handful of commits at the position including a pair of four-stars they have added arguably their most impactful piece with the commitment of Moss.</p>\n<p>A big man with elite arm length he presented as a prospect with excellent lower body flexibility and feet. His weight and physical upside were an early concern, but he has filled out and weighed in at 330 pounds this past March. There is likely a happy medium the Buckeyes strength staff can find for him, but this is a big body that can be a people mover. He can come off low, maintain a good base, drive his knees and generate push in the run game. We could see him initially working at guard earlier in his career, but with good agility and balance he has the tools to protect the edge. -- <em>Haubert</em></p>\n\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261610/hayden-stepp\">Hayden Stepp</a>, CB</h2>\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/2483.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 15</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/2483/oregon-ducks\">Oregon Ducks</a></p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: Oregon coach Dan Lanning picked up his latest high-profile defensive back talent when ESPN's No. 3 cornerback committed to the Ducks over Alabama and Cal on July 1.</p>\n<p>Stepp, an instinctive defender, is on track to be a four-year starter at Las Vegas powerhouse Bishop Gorman High School, where he logged 24 tackles and four pass breakups while lifting the program to a fifth consecutive state title last fall. Stepp took official visits to Alabama, Cal and Oregon, and also held significant spring interest from Georgia, before arriving as the lone five-star recruit and the top-ranked member of Lanning's latest recruiting class in Eugene.</p>\n<p>Oregon has consistently recruited elite defensive back talent since Lanning arrived in 2022, highlighted by 2025 breakout freshman <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/257191/brandon-finney\">Brandon Finney</a>. If Stepp ultimately signs later this year, he'll trail only Class of 2025 signee Na'eem Offord as Lanning's top-ranked cornerback addition with the Ducks. -- <em>Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: Stepp is another rare athlete with height, size, arm length and speed at corner. He is a very tall corner with great man to man skills where his long arms are used in press man-to-man situations and he can match up with big, strong targets on the jump ball. He looks like a safety. His combination of size, transition skills and sudden quickness really stand out. He posted a 4.45 40 and can play and be successful in any scheme due to his fluid hips and ability to cover ground and cut down throwing windows. -- <em>Luginbill</em></p>\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261599/eric-mcfarland-iii\">Eric McFarland III</a>, WR</h2>\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/245.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 16</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"014788cd-231c-e33e-32c3-7e8d9a1e530f\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/245/texas-a&m-aggies\">Texas A&M Aggies</a></p>\n\n\n<p><b>Background:</b> McFarland became the sixth five-star prospect to join Texas A&M's potentially historic 2027 class when he picked the Aggies over Georgia and Florida on June 28. The speedy, 5-foot-8 pass-catcher from Florida's IMG Academy reclassified into the 2027 class last December after hauling in 29 passes for 595 yards and 11 touchdowns as a sophomore last fall. He took official visits to Florida, Georgia, Tennessee and Texas A&M this spring, and McFarland also held significant interest from Miami and Ohio State. Recruited heavily by Texas A&M first-year offensive coordinator Holmon Wiggins, McFarland told ESPN that the program's coaching staff projects him to fill a similar role as Aggies junior <a data-player-guid=\"8866af06-1c09-3070-8669-ec7dfd8d017c\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/5079345/mario-craver\">Mario Craver</a>. McFarland could prove to be a record-setting pledge for Texas A&M. If the Aggies sign all six of their five-star commits in December, the program will set the record for five-star additions in the ESPN recruiting rankings era (since 2006), eclipsing Alabama's 2014 class and the Aggies' 2022 class, which each featured five five-star signees. <em>-- Lederman</em></p>\n\n\n<p><b>Scout's take:</b> McFarland is eerily similar to Miami's <a data-player-guid=\"4739f61f-078d-3703-8266-b827c113a066\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/5159175/malachi-toney\">Malachi Toney</a>, only McFarland is faster. The Aggies have a similar player in <a data-player-guid=\"20513dce-da6a-3e16-8e73-9d3f8e6e331c\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/5079326/terry-bussey\">Terry Bussey</a>, so they are adding another explosive offensive utility weapon who is an ideal player in the slot. He possesses elite-level speed, posting a 4.37 laser time in the 40-yard dash and is a home run threat with the ball in his hands in the open field at all times. One of McFarland's best attributes is his ability to separate and create space versus one-on-one man coverage. His short-area quickness and ability to rapidly accelerate compensate for his lack of ideal size. He is short, but not small. We could see him making an immediate contribution in the return game as well as sub-package offensive reps as he continues to grow and acclimate to the college level. Despite not having great size, his speed makes him a vertical threat as he can take the top off the defense. -- <i>Luginbill</i></p>\n\n\n<hr>\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/262014/monshun-sales\">Monshun Sales</a>, WR</h2>\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/84.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking: </strong>17</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to: </strong><a data-clubhouse-guid=\"bd06efe1-3050-2356-37c1-4b3fef1c29be\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/84/indiana-hoosiers\">Indiana Hoosiers</a></p>\n<p><strong>Background: </strong>Sales sealed a seismic in-state recruiting win for the defending national champions when ESPN's No. 4 wide receiver prospect committed to Indiana on July 17.</p>\n<p>The 6-foot-5, 200-pound recruit from Indianapolis is the state of Indiana's highest-ranked recruit since Jaylon Smith in 2013, and he had plenty of high-profile suitors among finalists Texas, Alabama, Ohio State and LSU. Despite a significant late push from the Longhorns, Sales chose to stay home with the Hoosiers, handing Indiana coach Curt Cignetti a potentially historic pledge and the latest highlight in the third-year coach's remarkable program turnaround. If Sales signs later this year, he'll surpass Class of 2022 signee Dasan McCullough (No. 43 in the 2022 SC Next 300) as the highest-ranked addition in school history.</p>\n<p>A two-way star at Indianapolis' Lawrence North High School, Sales caught 37 passes for 794 yards and nine touchdowns during his junior season last fall, adding 56 tackles on defense. <em>-- Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take: </strong>Indiana is getting one of those rare prospects who can really run and also has terrific size. At 6-foot-4, Sales has posted a 21.9 200 meters and 10.8 100 meters on the track in 2025. Indiana has had tremendous success with bigger targets on the outside in former Hoosier and current Baltimore Raven Elijah Surratt and current Hoosier Charlie Becker. Sales also brings physicality, body coordination and the ability to make plays on the jump ball. When you combine the speed and the height advantage, Sales will add to Indiana's vertical passing game in a big way. This commitment further accentuates the change in narrative and perception of Indiana's program over the past two seasons. When you win the national championship, your access to upper-tier high school prospects increases incrementally. <em>-- Luginbill</em></p>\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261613/raylaun-henry\">Raylaun Henry</a>, CB</h2>\n\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/245.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 18</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: Texas A&M Aggies</p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: Henry is the highest-ranked of six SC Next 300 recruits who joined the Aggies' latest class last fall as the program began picking up momentum in the 2027 cycle.</p>\n<p>The 6-foot-1, 180-pound recruit plays on one of the nation's top high school defenses at Baltimore power St. Frances Academy, where Henry shares the field with Sweeney, the Texas Tech commit, and fellow top 300 defensive back <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/266492/jernard-albright\">Jernard Albright</a> (Florida State pledge).</p>\n<p>He committed to Texas A&M over Alabama and Ohio State last November and staved off flip interest from both school earlier this year. If the Aggies ultimately secure his signature later this year, it'll mark Texas A&M's second consecutive cycle with a five-star cornerback after the program signed top-ranked athlete <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/257193/brandon-arrington\">Brandon Arrington</a> in the 2026 class. -- <em>Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: Henry is like a pest in coverage -- you can't get rid of him. With his his size and physicality. Texas A&M is getting an ideal boundary corner. He really likes to mix it up and challenge receivers at the line of scrimmage. For a player of his length, he has really smooth hips and can really run stride for stride and stay in phase with fast targets. Henry's aggressive style of play also shows up in run support. He plays the game with confidence and passion and has a very competitive temperament. The Aggies definitely have a type at corner and Henry checks those boxes. -- <em>Luginbill</em></p>\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261503/xavier-sabb\">Xavier Sabb</a>, WR</h2>\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/2483.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 19</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: Oregon Ducks</p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: Sabb, the reigning New Jersey Gatorade Football Player of the Year, followed Stepp as Oregon's second five-star pledge in the 2027 class.</p>\n<p>The 6-foot, 190-pound prospect from Glassboro, New Jersey, is the brother of Alabama starting safety <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/259223/keon-sabb\">Keon Sabb</a> and Crimson Tide freshman <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/256614/amari-sabb\">Amari Sabb</a>. He took official visits to Alabama, LSU, Oregon, Tennessee and UCLA before announcing his commitment to the Ducks on July 3. A two-way standout, Sabb will play wide receiver at the next level. He caught 59 passes for 897 yards and 13 touchdowns as a junior last fall while helping guide Glassboro to a second consecutive state title.</p>\n<p>Oregon coach Dan Lanning has signed at least a pair of five-star prospects in each of the last two cycles, and the Ducks are on track again in 2027 with Sabb and Stepp in the fold this summer. -- <em>Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: Sabb is a true weapon with the ball in his hands. He's really improved his stock with added bulk and strength to what was a very wiry, lean frame two years ago. He makes plays in space and can turn underneath routes into 80-yard home runs. This will also enhance his ability as a return specialist. He is very similar to former Georgia standout wide receiver Mecole Hardeman. Oregon is getting a game-changer in the open field who can play inside or outside and take the top off the defense with his 4.49 speed. -- <em>Luginbill</em></p>\n\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261549/honor-faalave-johnson\">Honor Fa'alave-Johnson</a>, S</h2>\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/30.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 20</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"63d7a1b4-ee19-f0ae-33c5-42a14e000fb8\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/30/usc-trojans\" target=\"_new\">USC Trojans</a></p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: After signing the nation's No. 1 class and more in-state recruits than in any cycle since 2011 last fall, USC has kept its foot on the gas with local prospects like Fa'alave-Johnson in the 2027 class.</p>\n<p>The Trojans made ESPN's top-ranked safety a priority in the early months of 2026 before beating Oregon, Texas, Miami, LSU and Notre Dame to Fa'alave-Johnson's pledge on March 14. A two-way standout at San Diego's Cathedral Catholic High School, the 6-foot, 175-pound recruit was credited with 40 tackles and three interceptions while totaling 1,530 rushing yards and 30 total touchdowns in his junior season last fall. Fa'alave-Johnson, one of nine in-state commits in USC's latest class as of mid-April, is expected to play on both sides of the ball when he joins the Trojans in 2027. <em>-- Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: USC wants more speed, versatility and playmaking on the perimeter, and Fa'alave-Johnson checks every box. A 10.6 100-meter sprinter with good length and a wiry frame, he brings true top-end speed to a defense that's being reshaped around range and takeaways. In the Trojans' secondary, he projects as a versatile player who can play high, roll down or match up in space. Fa'alave-Johnson's quickness, ball skills and open-field elusiveness also give USC a potential difference-maker in the return game. In a league in which explosive plays and hybrid defenders are at a premium, his combination of speed, physicality and versatility fits exactly what USC is trying to build on the back end.<em>-- Luginbill</em></p>\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/265138/zyron-forstall\">Zyron Forstall</a>, DE</h2>\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/245.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 21</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: Texas A&M Aggies</p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: The speedy edge rusher from New Orleans took spring visits to Miami, Notre Dame and Miami before sealing his place as the top-ranked defender the Aggies' star-studded 2027 class on March 31.</p>\n<p>Forstall, a 6-foot-4, 235-pound defensive end, began his high school career at Jesuit High School in New Orleans before transferring to Florida's IMG Academy ahead of his junior season last fall, where he posted 28 tackles, five sacks and four hurries. A versatile edge prospect capable of playing multiple positions, Forstall will mark the program's highest-rated defensive line addition under third-year coach Mike Elko if he signs later this year. <em>-- Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: In a crowd of top defenders, Forstall is not going to stand out physically, but when the ball is snapped he will grab your attention. He has a good frame and length with room to add more mass and muscle. In action, he has an explosive first step, keeps excellent pad level and is strong at the point of attack. He's functionally strong and his leaner build allows him to both set the edge and win as a pass-rusher. He has also demonstrated a businesslike approach to his craft and coachability that has drawn strong camp reviews. His initial impact could be more in a pass-rushing role in which he can get off the ball and upfield. Forstall has the tools to become a versatile centerpiece of Texas A&M's front seven as he continues to develop his frame. <em>-- Haubert</em></p>\n\n\n<hr>\n\n\n<h2><a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/264718/kamarui-dorsey\">Kamarui Dorsey</a>, S</h2>\n\n\n<p><img alt=\"\" class=\"floatleft\" src=\"https://a.espncdn.com/combiner/i?img=/i/teamlogos/ncaa/500/245.png&h=60&w=60\" width=\"30\"></p>\n<p><strong>2027 SC Next ESPN 300 ranking</strong>: 22</p>\n<p><strong>Committed to</strong>: Texas A&M Aggies</p>\n<p><strong>Background</strong>: The Aggies' first five-star commit in the 2027 class, Dorsey is ESPN's second-ranked safety in the cycle.</p>\n<p>The 6-foot-3, 205-pound defensive back from Hampton, Georgia, chose the Aggies over Georgia and LSU on Nov. 1 in the late stages of a 61-tackle, four-interception junior season at Hampton High School in Georgia. Dorsey fielded spring flip interest from multiple programs, including in-state Georgia, LSU, Ohio State and Tennessee, but remains locked in with Texas A&M as a cornerstone within an impressive defensive back class alongside Henry and top-100 safety <a href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-sports/football/recruiting/player/_/id/261528/jayquan-snell\">JayQuan Snell</a>. <em>-- Lederman</em></p>\n<p><strong>Scout's take</strong>: Texas A&M is emphasizing size, range and versatility on the back end. At 6-foot-3 with 4.52 speed, Dorsey brings a true safety body type with the athleticism to support the run, match up in man and patrol deep zones. Dorsey can play high or in the box, anticipating routes in zone and using his length and ball skills to generate turnovers. Coaches rave about his leadership and communication -- he already operates like a QB of the secondary -- and he's not a finesse player, embracing physicality as a core trait. In Mike Elko's defense, Dorsey projects as a multirole safety who can erase space, clean up in the alley and give the Aggies a high-ceiling tone-setter on the back end. <em>-- Luginbill</em></p>\n\n\n\n\n","allowAMP":true,"allowAds":true,"allowComments":true,"allowCommerce":true,"allowContentReactions":true,"allowSearch":true,"isLiveBlog":false,"links":{"api":{"self":{"href":"https://content.core.api.espn.com/v1/sports/news/48543549"}},"app":{"sportscenter":{"href":"sportscenter://x-callback-url/showStory?uid=48543549"}},"mobile":{"href":"http://m.espn.go.com/ncf/story?storyId=48543549"},"web":{"href":"https://www.espn.com/college-football/recruiting/story/_/id/48543549/college-football-recruiting-2027-prospects-five-star-scouts-take"}},"premium":false},{"id":50168837,"nowId":"1-50168837","contentKey":"50168837-1-5-1","dataSourceIdentifier":"7a5149cd931cf","publishedkey":"50168837","type":"HeadlineNews","feedDisplayType":"Default","headline":"Cowboys Archer","slug":"todd-archer","contributor":{"id":1915,"description":"Todd Archer"}}],"keywords":["news","NFL","Dallas Cowboys","Todd Archer"],"story":"<p>FRISCO, Texas -- Looking for ways to improve a defense that is 31st in yards and 26th in points allowed through five games, the <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"b0fc8fb7-9dbe-e574-9008-6f0ee7c6b234\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/team/_/name/dal/dallas-cowboys\">Dallas Cowboys</a> are bringing in longtime NFL coach Mike Pettine as a senior defensive assistant to work with defensive coordinator Christian Parker.</p>\n<p>\"It's something we've been talking about for a long time,\" Cowboys head coach Brian Schottenheimer said. \"I think as we looked at everything, we had a chance to convince Pett that it made sense for him. I think he's excited about coming in and adding experience and value. The most important thing for us was making sure we got the right guy, and I feel very, very confident that we did.\"</p>\n<p>Parker approached Pettine, 60, about joining the staff in a similar role after he was hired in January, but Pettine had just announced his retirement. Schottenheimer said the move would have been made even if the Cowboys had beaten the previously winless <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"13e8b157-a75a-ff24-ae91-fa3d52ddf204\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/team/_/name/tb/tampa-bay-buccaneers\">Tampa Bay Buccaneers</a> on Thursday.</p>\n<p><alsosee></p>\n<p>From 2022 to 2025, Pettine served as assistant head coach with the <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"3db556c9-ed76-a7a5-4ccb-a92fc6db4334\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/team/_/name/min/minnesota-vikings\">Minnesota Vikings</a>. Before spending the 2021 season with the <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"19d73366-b5a5-cca2-a176-a2c024ce0bb3\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/team/_/name/chi/chicago-bears\">Chicago Bears</a> as a senior defensive assistant, Pettine had a four-year run as the <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"06cd3bcf-7a86-53d4-28e7-aa049dd36261\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/team/_/name/gb/green-bay-packers\">Green Bay Packers</a>' defensive coordinator.</p>\n<p>In 2019, Pettine gave Parker his first NFL job as a quality control coach. Schottenheimer and Pettine were previously on Rex Ryan's staff with the <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"732d3caf-b350-1e34-48c6-b7cebb4a0d88\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/team/_/name/nyj/new-york-jets\">New York Jets</a> from 2009 to 2011. Pettine also was head coach of the <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"539babc5-e5fb-753b-8ae1-c15032a91ef4\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/team/_/name/cle/cleveland-browns\">Cleveland Browns</a> for the 2014 and 2015 campaigns.</p>\n<p>Parker, 34, is in his first year as defensive coordinator after eight years of NFL experience. From 2024 to 2025, he was the <a data-clubhouse-guid=\"d1a9b001-1df7-fbd7-ae4c-6ca7065286ec\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/team/_/name/phi/philadelphia-eagles\">Philadelphia Eagles</a>' defensive passing game coordinator under Vic Fangio.</p>\n<p>Though Dallas players have lauded Parker's communication style and his system, the production has not followed.</p>\n<p>The Cowboys have the worst-ranked run defense (168.4 yards per game), and opposing quarterbacks have completed 72.3% of their passes. The Cowboys have yet to record an interception and have just five sacks.</p>\n<p>Four of Dallas' five opponents have had season highs in yardage, and the Cowboys have given up at least 20 points in the past 12 games dating to last season, which is tied for the longest streak in team history.</p>\n<p>Pettine can be a sounding board for Parker.</p>\n<p>\"The other thing that's important is when you're not coaching a position, it frees you up to do a lot of other things,\" Schottenheimer said. \"You're very holistic in your approach when you're not coaching a position. You're able to really study the league and the trends and how we want to adjust versus this and how we want to handle that. And so again, I think it worked out beautifully for us.\"</p>\n<p>In the spring, Pettine told ESPN that Parker served as his eyes in the coaches' booth when they were together in Green Bay.</p>\n<p>\"There were certain things I wanted from him that would be hard to see from the sideline, and he was always good with those things,\" Pettine said in the spring. \"He was good at 'What can we do?' and 'What can't we do?' Again, he was a QC with us, but he definitely had my ear.\"</p>\n<p>The Cowboys have a relatively young defensive staff. Defensive pass game coordinator Derrick Ansley has the most NFL experience at seven years. Inside and outside linebacker coaches Scott Symons and Chidera Uzo-Diribe are in their first year in the NFL.</p>\n<p>Beyond Schottenheimer and Parker, Pettine coached with Cowboys defensive line coach Marcus Dixon at Minnesota and was with defensive tackle <a data-player-guid=\"99fef066-3b7b-f035-c202-879446d10d7c\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/3122752/kenny-clark\">Kenny Clark</a> and edge rusher <a data-player-guid=\"b20731b5-5a7d-b655-f5a2-ceb2c7855b7f\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4046523/rashan-gary\">Rashan Gary</a> in Green Bay.</p>\n<p>\"When you bring someone into this role, you want it to be someone that the coordinator, the head coach trusts. Because otherwise, I could put two people in a room and say, 'OK, well he's in charge, but I want you to give him feedback,' and if they don't have any relationship, then how are they going to ever get anything done?\" Schottenheimer said. \"That doesn't help anybody. So, that's how it started, and Pett was excited about retirement. He's been doing this a long time, and as we've been talking about this over the past couple weeks, it began to make more sense for him.\"</p>\n<p>The addition of Pettine wasn't the only news involving the Cowboys' beleaguered defense. The players held a meeting Monday to discuss the issues that have cropped up recently.</p>\n<p>\"We're just looking at ourselves in the mirror, putting what we wanted our standard to be,\" linebacker <a data-player-guid=\"64944dd8-160f-0726-2b9d-4e82e22db36e\" href=\"https://www.espn.com/nfl/player/_/id/4362088/demarvion-overshown\">DeMarvion Overshown</a> said. \"We put that on the board and we went by bullet point, bullet point: Are we holding up to this? Are we being this? As a unit, how can we get closer? What is it? Just hearing from all of us. So, it really wasn't like a fire drill conversation. It was more so, 'What can I do to help you? What do I expect from you on Sundays or even Monday walk-throughs?'<br>\n<br>\n\"We got to talk to each other like that because the ship is only going to go as far as the weakest person on the boat. So as long as we all in this together, I think we're going to be great.\"</p>\n\n\n\n\n","allowAMP":true,"allowAds":true,"allowComments":true,"allowCommerce":true,"allowContentReactions":true,"allowSearch":true,"byline":"Todd href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/245/texas-a&m-aggies\">Texas A&M</a> coach Mike Elko expressed regret Monday for his comments after losing to <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/142/missouri-tigers\">Missouri</a> in which he referred to the website TexAgs and a portion of the Aggies' fanbase as a \"cesspool.\"</p>\n<p>After the Aggies dropped to 3-3 on Saturday with a 27-6 defeat at No. 14 Missouri, Elko was asked what his message would be to Texas A&M fans who might be worried. He said there is a difference between the fanbase and social media, with a nod to Billy Liucci, the executive editor and co-owner of TexAgs.</p>\n<p>\"There's one group that I really don't care much about. Billy's cesspool can go be Billy's cesspool,\" Elko said. \"Our fanbase: Stay with us, be behind us, we're going to build something that we told you we were going to build. We're in a really bad spot right now, but we'll work our way out of it.\"</p>\n<p>On Monday, with Liucci in attendance at his weekly news conference, Elko said the viral comment was made in the heat of the moment and came across in a \"way that I certainly regret.\"</p>\n<p><alsosee></p>\n<p>\"I think everybody knows I have a tremendous relationship with Billy,\" Elko said. \"I appreciate Billy. I appreciate him being here today. There's nothing about him and I that have ever been off since 2018. I think everybody knows I appreciate our fanbase and everything that they're about and everything that they mean to Texas A&M. Outside of that, I'll leave it alone.</p>\n<p>\"I regret the way it was taken. I regret the way it came out. I regret the way it ran. That certainly was never my intention. And certainly, throughout the course of that interview, at no point ever did I lend any blame to anything other than myself. I am the reason why this team is struggling. I have owned that since this has started. I continue to own that. It's my job to figure out how to get it better.\"</p>\n<p>TexAgs, an independent fan site, was founded in 1997. It is the largest entity providing daily coverage of Texas A&M athletics, hosts a message board for its thousands of subscribers, and partners with the university to create NIL opportunities for A&M student-athletes.</p>\n<p>In response to Elko's comments Saturday, Liucci posted on his X account later that afternoon that \"calling out an incredibly large portion of the loyal but also understandably frustrated fan base was a wildly unnecessary misstep.\"</p>\n<p>The Aggies (3-3, 1-3 SEC) were No. 8 in the preseason AP Top 25 after an 11-2 season and the program's first appearance in the College Football Playoff in Elko's second year. However, losses to <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/96/kentucky-wildcats\">Kentucky</a>, <a href=\"https://www.espn.com/college-football/team/_/id/99/lsu-tigers\">LSU</a> and Missouri along with persistent struggles on offense have jeopardized their CFP ambitions.</p>\n<p>Quarterback <a data-player-guid=\"7ef5b217-b78c-3d9d-b93a-27aff33023de\" href=\"http://www.espn.com/college-football/player/_/id/4870971/marcel-reed\">Marcel Reed</a> ranks 15th among SEC starters in passing yards per game (182.8) with six passing touchdowns, six interceptions and the No. 92 QBR (45.5) in FBS. He is leading an offense that has put up six points in each of its past two losses. Elko called it a \"go-back-to-the-drawing-board moment again\" for an offense that must find ways to get more creative and efficient going forward.</p>\n<p>Elko also said Monday he had handed over defensive playcalling duties to D-coordinator Lyle Hemphill following the Aggies' 35-6 loss at LSU to take on a CEO role and be more helpful in addressing the team's problems.</p>\n<p>\"That was an important step just so I could have the freedom to be involved in everything,\" Elko said. \"I just think that was probably to the detriment of us for a couple weeks -- LSU, Kentucky -- trying to do too many things and probably not doing any of them well, to be honest.\"</p>\n<p>Texas A&M will face an FCS opponent, <a
